Thus, in this verse, in this Puranam, Sekkizhar Peruman for the first time uses the sacred name of Appoothi Adikal Nayanar. Although he had mentioned Appoothi Adikal's name earlier in the Puranam of Thirunavukkarasu Nayanar, Sekkizhar Peruman continues this Puranam. Therefore, Sekkizhar Peruman, who had not mentioned the Nayanar's name until now, places the sacred name of Appoothi Adikal Nayanar for the first time in this seventh verse, in the words of those who knew the pandal, in the words of the people, as an answer to Appar Swamigal. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's question was "Who?" In response, those people mention Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's name along with all his other good qualities. Furthermore, they say that Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, besides establishing the water pandal, also established groves, ponds, and reservoirs for everyone, for all beings, at all times, to quench their thirst without needing anyone else's help, and to help crops. Thus, those people tell Appar Swamigal all about his greatness. Hearing this from the people, Appar Swamigal asks where Appoothi Adikal Nayanar is. The people continue to answer. Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 04 — Appoothi Adikal Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 507, composed by Sekkizhar Swamigal. Thirunindra Sarukkam, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar Puranam. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman asks where Appoothi Adikal is. This is because Appoothi Adikal Nayanar is performing all these charities in the name of Thirunavukkarasu Nayanar, so there must be some reason for it. Thinking about what that reason might be, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman asks where Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's village is and where his house in that village is. By asking where Appoothi Adikal Nayanar is, the people think that this devotee wishes to see Appoothi Adikal Nayanar. Therefore, the people tell Thirunavukkarasu Nayanar all the information. When a question is asked, the answer given should be complete, so that no further questions arise regarding the matter. This is the way wise people answer. Following that method, the people there tell Thirunavukkarasu Peruman all the information about Appoothi Adikal. Moreover, their love for Shiva devotees is also revealed through this. The people answer the question asked by Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. "Thundriya nool maarbarum iththol padhiyar manaiyin kan sendranar ippozhuthu athuvum seyththandru naniththu endraar." The people reply, "Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, who wears the sacred thread on his chest, belongs to this ancient town, Thingalur. He has just now gone to his house. His house is also not very far; it is very close." By calling Appoothi Adikal Nayanar "Thundriya nool maarbar" (one with the sacred thread on his chest), they indicate that Appoothi Adikal Nayanar belongs to the Brahmin community. They say he belongs to "iththol padhi" (this ancient town). So, they state that Appoothi Adikal Nayanar belongs to this town and that he has just now gone to his house. They imply that until now, he himself was here at this water pandal, doing what was necessary for the devotees. Thus, they reveal the nature of Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's charity, his simplicity, and his love in their answer. They further say that Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's house is very close, pointing it out, and thus answer Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. Hearing this, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman goes to Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's house. Sekkizhar Peruman sings about it as follows: "Angu agandru munivarum poi Appoothi Adigalaar thangu manai kadaiththalai mun saarbaaga ullirundha Thingalur maraiththalaivar sezhungadaiyil vandhu adainthaar. Nangal piraan thamar oruvar enak kettunanninaar." This is the verse. Hearing the people's reply, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, the sage, goes from there and reaches the entrance of Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's house. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman arrives in front of Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's sacred house. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar hears that Thirunavukkarasu Peruman has come in front of his house. He has just now entered the house. Yet, hearing that a Shiva devotee has come in front of the house, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar immediately comes out of the house. Even though he has just come inside from outside, he disregards his task and fatigue, and upon hearing that a devotee has come, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar comes out of the house. This shows the depth of Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's devotion to devotees. Furthermore, there is also a hint here that his greatness, which was hidden from the world until now, is about to be revealed. Those around Appoothi Adikal Nayanar were also excellent in their devotion. That is why they immediately inform Appoothi Adikal about the arrival of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, the sage. Hearing that, Appoothi Adikal comes out. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, having come out, bows to the sacred feet of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. This reveals Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's nature of worshipping the sacred form as Shiva himself. "Neyam malindhavar vedamum aalayam thaanum aranenath thozhume" is the 12th sutra of Sivagnanabodham. According to that, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar bows to the sacred feet of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. Before Thirunavukkarasu Peruman bows to his feet, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar humbly speaks before Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, who also bows. According to the Kural, "Yaavarkkum nandraam panidhal avarullum selvarakke selvam thagaiththu" (Humility is good for all; among them, it is wealth for the wealthy), humility is for all devotees. "Thaazhvu enum thanmaiyodu saivamam samayam saarum oozh" (The destiny to attain the Saiva religion with humility) is from Sivagnana Siddhiyar. Thus, the action appropriate for devotees is to humbly bow to other devotees. This is especially appropriate for Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, who is the king of devotees. Therefore, Sekkizhar Peruman shows us Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's nature of bowing when he sees devotees, by saying, "Kadithu anaindhu Vageesar kazhal paniyamatru avartham adipaniyaa munpaniyum arasu." Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, who came out of the house upon hearing that a Shiva devotee had arrived, seeing the sacred attire of the devotee, proceeds to bow to the devotee. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, who has even greater love for devotees than the devotee himself, bows to Appoothi Adikal Nayanar even faster than him. We should understand this as both bowing to each other at the same time. This precedence in bowing is a measure of the depth of love for devotion. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, having bowed to Thirunavukkarasu Peruman in this manner, finally says, "Mudivil thavam seythaen kol munpozhiyum karunai puri vadivudaiyeer en manaiyil vandhu arulittru en endraar." Thus, Sekkizhar Peruman composes the verse. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar says to Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, "Only if I had performed great penance, boundless penance, would devotees like you be blessed to grace my home. But what penance have I performed for you to grace my home?" Appoothi Adikal Nayanar says this. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, without seeing Thirunavukkarasu Peruman directly, has been living by meditating on Thirunavukkarasu Peruman and contemplating his sacred name. That itself became a great penance for Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, and the fruit of that penance is now realized by Thirunavukkarasu Peruman gracing his home. This is expressed through Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's sacred utterance, unknowingly. When the Nayanar says, "Mudivil thavam seythaen kol" (Have I performed endless penance?), by saying "endless penance," it is also implied that he continues to perform that penance until he attains the feet of Shiva. This penance performed by Appoothi Adikal Nayanar includes penance performed in previous births and in this birth. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar addresses Thirunavukkarasu Peruman as "Munpozhiyum karunai puri vadivudaiyeer" (Oh, one with a form overflowing with grace). Here, the true indication of the depth of grace is revealed: Thirunavukkarasu Peruman himself came to Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's house without Appoothi Adikal Nayanar having to seek him. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, looking at Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, says with wonder, "Oh great one, whose sacred face overflows with grace, it seems I must have performed boundless penance in the past for you to grace my home." Hearing that, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman replies to Appoothi Adikal Nayanar.

He says, this is the next verse. "Oru kundravillaarai Thirupazhanaththul irainji varugindrom vazhikkaraiyil neer vaiththa vaaindha valam tharugindra nizhal thanneer pandharum kandu aththagaimai purigindra aram piravum kettanainthom ena pugazhvaar." This is the verse composed by Sekkizhar Peruman. To Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, who asked, "Have you graced my wife?", Navukkarasu Peruman replies, "We are coming here after worshipping Lord Shiva, who bent the single great Meru mountain as a bow, at Thirupazhanam. On the way, we saw the well-established and prosperous water pandal you have set up, which provides shade. We also heard that you are performing many other charities of that kind. We heard about you. Having seen and heard about the charitable deeds you perform, we have come to your sacred wife." Thus, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman replies. Saying this, he further tells Appoothi Adikal Nayanar. This is the next verse, composed by Sekkizhar Peruman. "Aaraniyum sadaimudiyar adiyarku neer vaiththa eeril perum thanneer pandharil nun per ezhudhaadhe veroru per mun ezhudha vendiya kaaranam en kol koorum ena edhir mozhindhaar kodhil mozhi kotravanaar." This is a wonderful verse composed by Sekkizhar Peruman. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman asks Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, "In that immensely great water pandal you have established for the devotees of Lord Shiva, who has a sacred matted hair adorned with the Ganga, why have you written another name instead of your own? Tell me the reason." Thus asks Navukkarasu Peruman, the king of flawless speech. When he says, "You have established a water pandal to help the devotees of Lord Shiva," the water pandal is described as "eeril perum thanneer pandhal" (immensely great water pandal) in this verse. "Eeril perum" describes the excellence of that water pandal. Sekkizhar Peruman uses "eeril perum thanneer pandhal" in this verse, as the utterance of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, to indicate the greatness that this water pandal itself becomes a means to liberation. Those who perform charities usually publicize their names at the place where they perform the charity. But Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, in the water pandal he established for devotees, has written the sacred name of Thirunavukkarasu Nayanar instead of his own. Why is that? asks Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, the king of flawless speech. Hearing these words of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar is about to become angry. Even though these words cause Appoothi Adikal Nayanar to become angry, it is implied that these are not harmful words. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman is skilled in speaking only harmless words. That is why Sekkizhar Peruman praises Thirunavukkarasu Peruman as "kodhil mozhi kotravanaar" (king of flawless speech) in this verse. As soon as Thirunavukkarasu Peruman says that the name Navukkarasu is another name, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar becomes enraged, he gets angry. The name Navukkarasu was bestowed upon Thirunavukkarasu Peruman by the Lord. It is the name bestowed by the Lord, "Ulagu ezhilum nin naamangal nayappura nannuga" (May your names be cherished in all seven worlds). Appoothi Adikal Nayanar gets angry that the devotee who came here has referred to that name Navukkarasu as another name. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, who does not know that the one who came is Thirunavukkarasu Peruman himself, gets angry. Sekkizhar Peruman describes this in the next verse. Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 05 — Appoothi Adikal Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 508, composed by Sekkizhar Swamigal. Thirunindra Sarukkam, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar Puranam. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman asks Appoothi Adikal Nayanar why he has written another name instead of his own in the charities and water pandals he establishes. As soon as he asks, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar loses his patience and becomes angry. He becomes extremely angry. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar is there with a disturbed mind, unable to bear hearing Thirunavukkarasu Nayanar's name being called another name. Generally, people in the world, whoever they may be, should practice patience. They should not lose their mental composure. They should never deviate from that state of patience. Especially, Shiva devotees should always maintain a tradition of unwavering minds. Vageesar's sacred utterance says, "Malaiye vandhu vidinum manithargal nilainindru kalangapperuthirel thalaivanagiya Eesan thamargalai kolaikkaiyanai thaan kondridukirkume" (Even if a mountain falls, if humans stand firm and do not waver, the Lord, their master, will kill the elephant with the murderous hand). Therefore, even if a mountain falls, one should not waver or deviate from the state of patience. This is a general worldly dharma, and it is especially a state that Shiva devotees should maintain. Not only that, one Shiva devotee should be very loving and desirous towards another Shiva devotee in front of that Shiva devotee. One should speak hesitantly, bow, and stand humbly. Even after knowing that the visitor is a Shiva devotee, Appoothi Adikal deviates from his state, abandons his patience, and becomes angry. Why? Because Nandi Devar asks the Lord for the boon of a mind that cannot tolerate the defamation of Vedas and Shaivism as the first boon. According to that principle, one should not tolerate Shaiva defamation. According to that rule, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar becomes angry here. Here, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar considers calling the sacred name Thirunavukkarasar another name as a great defamation. Therefore, his mind loses its composure, he loses patience, and thus becomes angry. If anyone defames Lord Shiva, Lord Shiva's devotees, or Shiva scriptures, one should not listen to it. One should not remain silent after listening. Listening is a sin, and remaining silent after listening is also a sin. Sakthi Nayanar cut off the tongues of those who defamed Shiva. But in these days, what is called the Shaiva world seems to remain silent without any agitation in their hearts even when they hear or see Shiva defamation. Moreover, it also commits Shiva defamation in many ways. This is the cruelty of time. Therefore, in this situation, it is very difficult for us in this era to understand the philosophy of the mindset that Appoothi Adikal Nayanar had. Generally, if someone hears themselves or their wife, children, or relatives being defamed, how much anger and rage they show, causing quarrels and resisting. But if one hears the living God and the devotees of God, who are our spiritual relatives, being defamed, how great a folly and sin it is to remain completely calm and silent, writes Sivakavimani Ayya at this point. Therefore, according to the boon asked by Nandi Devar that one should not remain silent after hearing defamation of Vedas and Shiva, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar loses patience and becomes angry, considering calling the sacred name Thirunavukkarasar another name as a great defamation. He becomes angry and replies. He begins by saying, "You spoke well." Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, even though he was in a state of disturbed mind, due to his love for the sacred attire of the devotee, says auspiciously, "You have spoken well." Appoothi Adikal Nayanar says, "You spoke well. Is the sacred name of that great person, our Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, who, with the support of his deep devotion to the Lord, overcame all the conspiracies made by Pallava in collaboration with the shameless and cruel Jains, another name?" Thus, he expresses his anger. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's mental fortitude is evident from his own sacred utterance, "En kadan pani seythu kidaththal, than kadan adiyenaiyum thaanguthal" (My duty is to serve and remain, His duty is to sustain even me), with the support of the depth of his sacred service rooted in the sacred feet of Lord Shiva. We had extensively contemplated in the Puranam of our Thirunavukkarasu Nayanar how such a great person, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, overcame the conspiracies of the Jains. Therefore, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, having heard all those incidents, develops great devotion to Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. His devotion to Thirunavukkarasu Peruman matures by repeatedly hearing about him. Therefore, for that reason, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar becomes angry, he gets enraged. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar continues to speak. Sekkizhar Peruman describes it in the next verse. Sekkizhar Peruman continuously presents two verses: "Nammai udaiyavar kazhal keezh nayandha thiruththondalae immaiyilum pizhaippadhena enpolvaarum theliya semmai puri Thirunavukkarasar thiruppeyar ezhudha vemmai mozhi yaan ketka vilambineer ena vilambi Pongu kadal kal mithappil pontherum avar perumai Anganartham bhuvanaththil ariyaathaar yaarularea Mangalamaam thiruvedaththudan nindru ivvagai mozhindheer Enguraiveer neer thaam yaar iyambum ena iyambinaar." Appoothi Adikal Nayanar says, "By the sacred service performed with devotion at the feet of the Lord who owns us, even in this birth, we shall be saved. So that even those like me may understand this clearly, I live by taking the sacred name of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, who performs righteousness, as the name for everyone everywhere. Why do you speak such cruel words? It has come to pass that I have to hear such cruel words," he replies. Lord Shiva is our owner. Lord Shiva is the master who owns you, me, and all other living beings as His servants. He is the one who accompanies us in this life and the next, granting salvation. He is the one who helps living beings in this life as well. Thus, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar says, "I have written the sacred name of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman so that even those like me may clearly understand that only by performing sacred service at the feet of Lord Shiva can we attain salvation even in this life." He says, "I have written the sacred name of Thirunavukkarasu, who performs righteousness, so that even those like me, who are dull-witted and of limited intelligence, may understand." Here, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar speaks of himself as "dull-witted and of limited intelligence," humbling himself. This reveals that Appoothi Adikal Nayanar was utterly devoid of ego and adorned himself with the humility of Shaivism. He says that our Thirunavukkarasu Peruman performed righteousness so that even ignorant worldly people might know. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar explains that Thirunavukkarasu Peruman performed righteousness so that even dull-witted people like him could understand that only by performing sacred service at the feet of Lord Shiva can we attain salvation even in this life. This is because Thirunavukkarasu Peruman was a great person who overcame all the evil deeds of the Jains solely through the power of his devotion to Shiva. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar is always immersed in the memories of the righteous deeds performed and demonstrated by Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, such as "Eesan endhai inaiyadi neezhale" (The shade of the twin feet of my Lord God), "Endrum oruvar thamar naam anjuvathu yaathondrum illai anja varuvathum illai" (We are always His servants, we fear nothing, and nothing will come to make us fear), "Adiporundha kai thozha natthunaiyavadhu Namasivayave" (Namasivaya is the good help to worship with folded hands), and "Etha vallar thamakku idukkan illaiye" (Those who can praise thus have no suffering). Therefore, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar gives this reply from that state. Sundaramurthy Swamigal has praised this righteousness of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman in Thiruthondathogai as "Thirunindra semmaiye semmaiya konda Thirunavukkaraiyan." Therefore, the implication is that Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's life occurred for the salvation of all people in the world. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar continues, "Is there anyone in this world, created by Lord Shiva, who does not know the greatness of that great person who crossed the turbulent sea on a stone raft? You are in the auspicious sacred attire of Shaivism. Yet, you speak in this manner," asks Appoothi Adikal Nayanar. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar here recounts the sacred history where, according to the Jain conspiracy and the king's order, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman was tied to a large stone and thrown into the sea, but that stone itself became a raft and floated. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar cites this sacred history as an example of the extraordinary greatness of the miracles performed by Thirunavukkarasu Peruman in his sacred history. Are there any in this world who do not know these acts of divine grace performed by Thirunavukkarasu Peruman? He says, "No one." Sekkizhar Peruman composes this in the verse as "Anganartham bhuvanaththil ariyaathaar yaarularea." "Anganartham bhuvanam" means the 224 worlds created and destroyed by Lord Shiva, the compassionate one, for the salvation of souls. In the history of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, these acts of divine grace performed by Lord Shiva, these acts bestowed by the Lord, are known in all worlds. Especially, you are in the auspicious sacred attire. So, he asks, "Even though you are in this Shaiva sacred attire, you speak like this?" The Shaiva sacred attire is an auspicious sacred attire. But what you say is contrary to that auspiciousness, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar speaks with this implication here. He does not explicitly state the inauspicious word, which is the opposite of auspiciousness, nor does he connect the inauspiciousness with the sacred attire. He just says, "You speak like this." This place is also an example that inauspicious words never came from the sacred utterance of our Sekkizhar Peruman. This is conveyed by the place where Appoothi Adikal Nayanar asks, "Mangalamaam thiruvedaththudan nindru ivvagai mozhindheer" (You, standing in auspicious sacred attire, spoke in this manner). Without using any word opposite to auspiciousness, he implies, "You speak in this way." Having said all this, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar angrily asks our Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, "Who are you? Where do you reside? Tell me." Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 06 — Appoothi Adikal Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 509, composed by Sekkizhar Swamigal. Thirunindra Sarukkam, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar Puranam. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar asks, "You call Thirunavukkarasu Nayanar's name another name, and that too while in Shiva's attire. Who are you? Where do you come from? Where do you reside? Please tell me." To this, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman replies. Sekkizhar Peruman composes the verse: "Thiru maraiyor adhu mozhiya Thirunavukkarasar avar perumai arindhu urai seyvaar. Pira thuraiyin nindru era arulu perunjoolaiyinaal aatkollu adaindhu uyndha therulum unarvilladha sirumaiyen yaan endraar." As soon as Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, the Brahmin radiating divine splendor, spoke thus, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, considering Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's greatness, the greatness of his love, and the greatness of his devotion to the Lord's sacred service, replies. He says, "Pira thuraiyin nindru era arulu perunjoolaiyinaal aatkollu adaindhu uyndha therulum unarvilladha sirumaiyen yaan." That is, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman replies, "I am the humble one who, by the grace of the Lord, was afflicted by the great colic disease, which was the means to receive the Lord's grace, in order to rise from the external path of Jainism, and thus was taken by the Lord, attained Him again, and was saved, but I lack clear understanding." He refers to Jainism as an "external path." All religions serve as ghats (steps) according to their respective levels. But all those paths are not the path that leads souls to salvation. Therefore, the colic disease was bestowed upon Thirunavukkarasu Peruman by the Lord to help him rise from Jainism, which is one of the external religions. We see that colic disease as the embodiment of grace in many places. We saw it praised as "Vazhvu pera tharu soolai" (colic that grants life) in the 1338th verse. It is the nature of the wise to consider and accept that sufferings like disease, poverty, etc., are manifestations of the Lord's grace to purify and save souls. Our Thirunavukkarasu Peruman also considers it so. He considers the colic disease as the Lord's grace. Thus, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman says, "I am the one who, having been taken by the Lord's grace, came back to this Shaiva religion." Thirunavukkarasu Peruman refers to himself as "therulum unarvilladha sirumaiyen" (a humble one lacking clear understanding). This is because, as we saw in the Puranam of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, he was born into a family that followed the Shaiva religion from generation to generation. Yet, without realizing its greatness and without understanding the good and bad of religions, he joined Jainism, an external religion, and reviled Shiva. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman refers to this humility as "therulum unarvilladha sirumaiyen." We saw that Thirunavukkarasu Peruman sang with this as the inner meaning in Elaith Thiruthandagam, Thiruvarur Thiruviratham, and many other utterances. The previous verse was where Appoothi Adikal Nayanar spoke of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's greatness. There, he praised the stone floating in the sea as Thirunavukkarasar's greatness. Yet, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman lived by considering himself a humble one lacking understanding. This humility is the greatness of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. What Appoothi Adikal Nayanar saw and wondered at as Thirunavukkarasar's greatness, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman saw as his own humility. We can see this in his own sacred utterances in many places. Thus, Thirunavukkarasu Nayanar considered that Appoothiyar's greatness lay in his own humility. Thus, living by recognizing one's humility is wisdom and the path to salvation. Without this, knowing that "these are all my greatness" is the nature of ordinary people, which greatly increases ego and leads to downfall. Here, Appar Swamigal declares himself to be a humble one lacking clear understanding. This is not said as a formality like a humble submission. This is truly the path that Appar Peruman followed, realizing it from within. Many of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's heartfelt prayers to the Lord, such as "Pithane pedaiyen peyen nayen pizhaithanaigal aththanaiyum poruththai andre" (Oh Madman, I am foolish, a devil, a dog, you forgave all my faults), serve as evidence for this. Thus, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman says, "I am that Thirunavukkarasu, I am the humble one who was taken by the Lord from an external religion and came to the Shaiva religion." Hearing that, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar realizes the truth. As soon as he realizes, his lotus hands automatically fold over his head. Tears stream from his eyes like a waterfall. His speech falters. His entire body is covered in goosebumps. He immediately falls to the ground, prostrates, and places the lotus feet of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman on his head. Sekkizhar Peruman composes this in the next verse. Sekkizhar Peruman beautifully describes in the verse: "Arasu ariya urai seyya Appoothi Adigal thaam kara kamala misai kuviya kannaruvi paainthizhiya urai kulari udambellam urumam pulagam pozhiya tharaiyin misai veezhndhu avartham sarana kamalam poondar." Appoothi Adikal Nayanar had not at all expected Thirunavukkarasu Peruman to be present in front of his house. He was unaware of it. He had not expected it. In this state of unexpectedness and unawareness, he suddenly received this great blessing: the one whose name he had always chanted has appeared before him. The one whom he had meditated upon until now stands before him. This sacred verse describes the mental state Appoothi Adikal Nayanar attained and the emotional expressions that appeared in him when he realized this great blessing that he suddenly received. How did Sekkizhar Peruman describe Appoothiyar's state in the previous verses 1795, 96, and 97? Sekkizhar Peruman, who described the anger he felt in his mind and the words he uttered as its manifestations when he heard Thirunavukkarasar's name being called another name, now in this verse, states that Appoothi Adikal Nayanar attains a completely different state. To depict this like a painting is a very rare and difficult task for a great painter. But to paint these states of the first meeting of these two great personalities as a living painting before our eyes is unique and easy for Sekkizhar Peruman's divine poetic skill. As soon as Thirunavukkarasu Peruman spoke thus, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's hands automatically fold over his head. Without any effort, his hands automatically fold over his head. This is not a habit that comes suddenly in a day. The habit of folding his hands over his head whenever he thought of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman and whenever he uttered Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's sacred name, now, without any effort, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's hands automatically rise above his head and offer salutations. He refers to them as Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's lotus hands. As soon as his hands fold, tears stream from his eyes. Tears stream due to the manifestation of love. Along with that, a continuous rain of tears pours. When love matures, speech falters. Similarly, here, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's speech falters. His hair stands on end. He immediately falls to the ground and places Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's sacred feet on his head. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar placed Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's lotus feet on his head. Sekkizhar Peruman continues to sing. "Matru avarai edhir vanangi Vageesar eduththarula attrvargal arunidhiyam petrar pol arumaraiyor mutra ulam kali koora munnindru kooththadi utra viruppudan soozha odinaar padinaar." This is the verse. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman also bows in return to Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, who was prostrate, having placed Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's lotus feet on his head, and lifts him up. It is stated in the verse that Thirunavukkarasu Peruman bowed in return to Appoothi Adikal Nayanar. A reciprocal bow means that he also bows to him just as Appoothi Adikal Nayanar prostrated. Even before this, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman bowed upon seeing Appoothi Adikal Nayanar. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar bowed upon seeing Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. That was a general salutation when they saw each other as Shiva devotees, even though they had not seen or known each other before. The salutations performed now are special salutations, performed after knowing and recognizing each other well. These salutations were performed when love blossomed between them. The first act should be to lift up the one who has prostrated. Before that, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman bows in return and then lifts him up. This shows us that Thirunavukkarasu Peruman stood in the true discipline that humbling oneself to love is the foremost duty. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar rejoices as if those suffering in poverty have found great wealth. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar becomes extremely happy, as if those suffering in poverty, who, no matter how much they search or try, find it difficult to obtain, have found a great treasure. His heart is filled with as much joy as it can hold. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar had performed penance considering this as the ultimate goal of his life. He had considered it his lifelong penance to have the darshan of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, his object of meditation. When the fruit of that ascetic life was realized, there was no greater blessing to be obtained than this. Therefore, for that reason, he rejoices greatly. He dances and sings in great joy. "Aasaiyodum Aran adiyar adiyarai adaindhittu avar karumam un karumamaaga seythu koosi mozhindhu arul gnanakkuriyin nindru kumbittu thattamittu kooththadi thiriye" is the second viruttam for the 12th sutra of Sivagnana Siddhiyar. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, having attained the grace of the guru, was a perfect one, having attained the state of Jivanmukta. Therefore, when he directly saw his Jnana Guru, he saw Thirunavukkarasu Peruman as the Lord Himself, in both the aspects of a devotee and a guru. Therefore, when he received the Lord's grace, all the actions of devotees, such as dancing and singing, occur here. "Aadugindrilai kooththudaiyan kazharkku anbu ilai enbu urugi paadugindrilai" (You do not dance, you have no love for the feet of the Lord of dance, you do not sing with your bones melting) is from Thiruvachagam. "Meiviravu perambu miguthiyinaal aaduthalum avviyalbil paaduthalumai nigazhvaar" (They dance and sing in that manner due to the abundance of true great love) is from Periyapuranam, 1055th verse. Thus, he dances and sings. He circumambulates the Peruman. Generally, those who circumambulate should walk slowly, step by step, according to the rules. But now, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar does not walk that way but runs very fast. That is due to the abundance of love. He sings the praises of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. He also sings as a song the rare blessing he has received. This indicates the singing of the melody that arises from within when the mind is filled with excessive joy. "Thirandu thirandu un thiru vaarthai virippaar ketpaar mechchuvaar" (They will gather and spread your sacred words, listen, and praise) is from Thiruvachagam. Thus, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar sings, dances, and praises the greatness of the Peruman. Sekkizhar Peruman then describes the actions Appoothi Adikal Nayanar performed due to this joy. Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 07 — Appoothi Adikal Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 510, composed by Sekkizhar Swamigal. Thirunindra Sarukkam, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar Puranam. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, filled with immense joy upon seeing Thirunavukkarasu Peruman gracing his home, rushes inside, not knowing what to do. There, he tells his wife, children, and other relatives the joyful news of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's arrival. The relatives who were in Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's house on the day Thirunavukkarasu Peruman arrived were indeed very fortunate relatives. Thus, filled with abundant love and joy, he brought all his relatives with him and came out of the house. The next verse is: "Manaiviyarudan makkal matrum ulla suttraththor anaivaraiyum kondu irainji aaraadha kaathaludan munaivarai ul ezhundharuluviththavar thaal mun vilakkum punai malar neer thangal mel theliththu ullum pooriththaar." Appoothi Adikal Nayanar first brings his wife, children, and all other relatives to worship. Here, the wife is given prominence, and it says, "with his wife and relatives." This is because the wife is an equal partner. Among relatives, the wife is primary. Furthermore, the wife has rights over the household. Therefore, serving Thirunavukkarasu Peruman is one of the wife's duties. To convey these special qualities, it is stated here that he first brought his wife, and then with her, his relatives, to worship Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. Just as Appoothi Adikal Nayanar was glorified, his wife was truly very special. Just as Appoothi Adikal Nayanar had that depth of love, concealing his son's death by snakebite and intending to offer sacred food to Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, so too his wife had abundant love and devotion for Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, which we will see in the upcoming history. Therefore, such a special wife is placed first, and it is stated that with her, his sons and relatives came to worship. It is understood from this that Appoothi Adikal Nayanar had many sons, as "makkal" (children) is used in the plural. It is stated here that he brought everyone, including relatives, to worship Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. Therefore, he also brought his household servants and others to worship there. After everyone had worshipped, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar welcomes Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. He brings Thirunavukkarasu Peruman into the sacred house, cleanses his sacred feet with water, etc., and performs foot worship. This is called 'paathyam'. Following 'paathyam', 'aasamanam' and 'arkkiyam' are the rituals in worship. Following these prescribed rituals, he first cleanses Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's feet with water, which is called 'paathyam'. Sekkizhar Peruman refers to this as 'malar punai neer' (water adorned with flowers). It is customary to cleanse the feet of the Lord and elders with water mixed with fresh flowers and fragrant substances for 'paathyam' and other rituals. Such water, used to cleanse the feet, becomes sacred water. Therefore, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar purified himself internally and externally with that sacred water. This is called 'pooriththal' (filling oneself with purity). Now the next verse. "Aasanaththil poosanaigal amarviththu viruppinudan vaasanirrai thiruneettruk kaappu endhi manam thazhaippa thesam uyya vandhavarai thiruvamudhu seivikkum nesamuravinnappam seyya avarum adhu nerndhaar." This is the verse. Thus, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar places a suitable plank for Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, who has entered the house, seats him on it, and worships him. This is called 'aasanam samarppiththal' in the worship procedure. This is the first part of the worship procedure. This is the method of placing the deity on the seat with appropriate mantras and bhavanas. Thus, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar performs worship by seating him on the asana. Offering incense, lighting lamps, performing archana, etc., are all prescribed as parts of Maheswara Puja. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar performs them with love. After thus placing him on the asana and performing these rituals, he holds up the sacred ash casket. The fragrance of civet, green camphor, etc., mixed with the sacred ash, is present there. Along with that, the natural fragrance of sacred ash is also present. Therefore, Sekkizhar Peruman refers to this as 'vaasanirrai thiruneeru' (fragrant sacred ash). Thus, holding up the sacred ash casket before Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, with overflowing love, requests Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, who has come for the salvation of the world, to partake of the sacred food. Bound by Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's love, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman also agrees. Now the next verse. "Sey thavar isaindha podhu thirumanaiyaarai nokki eydhiya peru nambaal irundhavaaru ennea endru maithigazh midattrinaan than arulinaal vandhathendre uyndhum endru vandhukondu thiruvamudhu aakkal utraar." Thirunavukkarasu Peruman is a great ascetic. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar considered his consent to partake of the sacred food as a great blessing. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, the great ascetic, gracing his home was one blessing. And his consent to partake of the sacred food was another blessing bestowed upon them, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar rejoices. These two great blessings were received by Appoothi Adikal Nayanar without any expectation. Therefore, he praises the grace of Lord Shiva, saying that these could not have been obtained without divine grace. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar is utterly devoid of any egoistic thought that this blessing was obtained as a result of his many days of meditation. He praises divine grace, saying that this blessing came through the grace of Lord Shiva. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar praises the grace of Lord Shiva, saying that this great blessing occurred due to divine grace, without any egoistic thought that he meditated on Thirunavukkarasu Peruman for so long, and therefore Thirunavukkarasu Peruman has graced his home as a result of that meditation. We have already seen that Appoothi Adikal Nayanar was utterly devoid of ego, as he performed charities in the name of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman without writing his own name in the alms-houses he established. Now it is further confirmed. Therefore, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar considered that this blessing came through divine grace. This is because penance itself does not yield results. It is the Lord who must bring about the fruit of the penance performed, which is the truth of Jnana Sastras. In the Puranam of Sakkiya Nayanar, the subtlety of "seyvinaiyum adhan payanum serppanum" (the deed, its fruit, and the one who bestows it) is elaborately explained. Thus, filled with great joy, he tells this to his wife. Therefore, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar and his wife performed their respective tasks to prepare sacred food for Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. Even in the Puranam of Ilaiyankudi Mara Nayanar, Ilaiyankudi Maran performs some tasks to prepare sacred food, and his wife performs some tasks. We can recall that here too. Sekkizhar Peruman continues to sing. "Thooya narkarigalana aruvagai suvaiyaal aakki aaya in amudhum aakki amudhu seythu arula thangal seyavar thammil mooththa Thirunavukkarasai vaazhai meya porkkuruththu kondu va ena viraindhu vittar." This is the next verse. To prepare sacred food for the Peruman, they cook pure, good curries with six tastes. What are pure curries? Vegetables, etc., used for Shiva Puja or Maheswara Puja, should be grown in pure soil. They should not be grown with impure manure, water, etc. Also, they should not be placed in impure vessels. Besides these, there are many other rules. All these are for the vegetables to be pure. Sekkizhar sings, "Thooya narkarigalal ana innamudhu" (sweet food made of pure good curries). We saw what pure curries, pure vegetables mean. Now Sekkizhar Peruman also mentions "nalla karigal" (good curries). Good vegetables mean they should not cause qualities like tamas. Those vegetables should not cause diseases by disagreeing with the humors like vata, pitta, and kapha. Moreover, they should be easily digestible. Thus, good vegetables should provide comfort to the body. They should also be conducive to intellectual clarity. And they should be appropriate for the place, time, and the eaters. To indicate that they should be according to all these rules, Sekkizhar Peruman mentions "Thooya narkarigalal ana." Furthermore, they prepare sweet food with six tastes using such pure, good vegetables. The six tastes are sweet, pungent, sour, salty, astringent, and bitter. With these six tastes, they prepare good, sweet food in four types of edible foods. In the Puranam of Ilaiyankudi Mara Nayanar, verse 443 in Periyapuranam, at the place "Undi naalu vidaththil aaru suvai thiraththinil," we have already seen in detail such types of cooked food. Similarly, here too, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar and his wife cook foods of six tastes in four types, making a total of 24 types of foods. Moreover, Sekkizhar Peruman refers to this as "in amudhu" (sweet food). "In amudhu" means it is made from "sennal" (red rice). We have already seen in the Puranam of Ilaiyankudi Mara Nayanar and Arivattaya Nayanar that the paddy used for Shiva Puja or Maheswara Puja should be "sennal." That is the best type suitable for good sacred food. Therefore, here too, they cook sacred food with rice made from "sennal." Why do we refer to it as "amudhu" (nectar)? Because "amudhu" protects from the suffering of hunger. Even the nectar of the gods got that name for this reason. Therefore, they refer to food as "like divine nectar" as a compliment. That is, just as those who consume nectar do not suffer from hunger and do not die, good food is referred to as "like divine nectar." But in reality, even for that divine nectar, that name is merely a compliment. Lord Shiva alone is nectar. He is the insatiable nectar. He is the eternal nectar. Thus, they prepare sacred food. Sekkizhar Peruman has arranged the cooking of vegetables first and then the cooking of rice, the sacred food. This is because the rice should be hot at the right consistency. To indicate the method of cooking curries first and then the sacred food, he sings about cooking vegetables first and then cooking the sacred food as "amudhamakki." Both of them perform this act of preparing sacred food: Appoothi Adikal Nayanar and his wife. Generally, preparing sacred food is the wife's duty, but due to love and devotion, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar also assists her by doing tasks suitable for him. After the sacred food is ready, they call one of their sons, the eldest Thirunavukkarasu, and send him to pluck a banana leaf for Thirunavukkarasu Peruman to partake of the sacred food. They send him saying, "Go quickly and bring a good, suitable banana shoot." We have already seen that Appoothi Adikal Nayanar named all his sons after Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. In this verse, they call the eldest Thirunavukkarasu and send him to pluck a banana leaf. Therefore, it is understood that he is the eldest among Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's sons. They call the eldest Thirunavukkarasu and tell him to quickly bring a "vaazhai meya porkkuruththu" (a golden banana shoot suitable for banana) for Thirunavukkarasu Peruman to partake of the sacred food. "Vaazhai meya porkkuruththu" means a young banana leaf. That banana leaf should be large, long, and not fully mature. These are its meanings. Only the shoot of the first banana plant will have such qualities. But if this first leaf is plucked, the benefit of that banana plant will be less, so people in the world do not pluck this first leaf. But here, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar sends him to pluck the first leaf of the banana plant. This is because Appoothi Adikal thinks that the greatest benefit of that banana tree is for it to be used for Thirunavukkarasu Peruman to partake of the food. That is why he says this. Thus, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar and his wife, the parents, send the eldest Thirunavukkarasu to quickly bring such a suitable banana leaf shoot. He also goes quickly. Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 08 — Appoothi Adikal Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 511. Thiruneetru Sarukkam, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar Puranam. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar and his wife send their eldest son, the elder Thirunavukkarasu, to pluck a banana leaf shoot for Thirunavukkarasu Peruman to partake of the sacred food. Sekkizhar Peruman composes the next verse. "Nalla thaai thandhai eva naan idhu seyyap perren endru ollaiyil viraindhu thottaththul pukku periya vaazhai mallal anguruththai eerum pozhudhinil vaal araavu ondru allal utru azhungich sorangaiththil theendittru andre." This is the verse composed by Sekkizhar Peruman. In the first part of this verse, Sekkizhar Peruman shows what the elder Thirunavukkarasu thinks in his mind as he goes to pluck the banana leaf. The elder Thirunavukkarasu rejoices in his heart and goes quickly, thinking, "I have received this blessing to obey the command of my good mother and father and perform this task." He thinks his mother and father are good parents because they engaged him in this service to the Lord's devotees and guided him. Therefore, they are doing good for him. So, the elder Thirunavukkarasu rejoices, thinking his mother and father are good parents. Generally, the primary duty of parents is to guide their children to the right path. Among them, the mother is the foremost in guiding her child to the right path. "Annaiyum pithavum munnari deivam" (Mother and father are the first known deities) is a moral saying. We should reflect on why the mother is mentioned first and the father second. Although "Eva makkal moova marundhu" (Children who obey are an unfailing medicine) is also a moral saying, it is a general rule. A parent may have many children. In such cases, it is the duty of parents to understand the time, place, the children's qualifications, and everything else, and to nurture and guide each child according to their needs. It is the duty of children to fulfill the commands given by their parents and follow that path. The elder Thirunavukkarasu here feels proud and happy. This is because Appoothi Adikal Nayanar and his wife have many children, and among them, he, the elder Thirunavukkarasu, has received this opportunity. This is the kind of elevated pride, "Aththan enakku aruliyavaaru aar peruvaar achchuve" (Who else will receive the grace my Lord bestowed upon me?), as in Thiruvachagam. This is not the egoistic pride that arises from "I." This is pride related to the specialty of devotion to the Lord. This pride will weaken ego. The task assigned to him by his parents is to bring the serving leaf for Thirunavukkarasu Peruman to partake of the sacred food. It is the task of plucking the leaf that will be placed for serving the sacred food. No matter how excellent the sacred food items are, the banana leaf, which serves as the seat and base for the sacred food, is what helps. In the worship procedure, the asana mantra is recited first, and we can compare the idea that the asana mantra comes first here. Therefore, the elder Thirunavukkarasu rejoices, considering its importance. Thus, with various thoughts, he goes to the task of plucking the banana leaf, rejoicing in himself. How does he go? Sekkizhar Peruman sings, "Ollaiyil viraindhu" (quickly and swiftly). "Ollai" also means swiftness. He has used two words for swiftness, "ollaiyil viraindhu." This is to indicate that the child goes very quickly. He goes very quickly and enters the garden next to his house. This is a common arrangement in the Chola country. Having a garden next to the house is a common arrangement in the Chola country. This is because the parents of the elder Thirunavukkarasu send him to bring the banana leaf after the sacred food is cooked, so that the serving leaf, the banana leaf, does not wilt and remains fresh. The elder Thirunavukkarasu thus enters the banana garden and sees a beautiful banana tree with good, abundant growth, and its beautiful banana shoot. He decides that this is the best and begins to cut it. At that moment, a snake bit him on his hand. It bit him on his palm. "Vaal araa ondru" is mentioned in this verse. "Vaal" means light. The eyes of a snake are luminous. Some types of snakes in Sri Lanka have 'drishti visham' (poison by sight). That is, just by looking, poison enters those who are looked at. Similarly, the fangs of a snake are very luminous. Moreover, snakes have multi-colored upper and lower skins on their bodies. Such a snake was on that banana tree. The snake was on the shoot of the banana tree from which the elder Thirunavukkarasu tried to cut the leaf. So, when he tried to cut the banana leaf, the snake that was there bit the elder Thirunavukkarasu's hand. Poisonous creatures sometimes reside in banana leaves, and their venomous properties can be present in the leaves. That is why there has been a tradition of washing leaves with water before eating. Sekkizhar Peruman then composes what happens next in the following three verses. Sekkizhar Peruman sings what happened there in these three verses: "Kaiyinil kavarndhu sutri kan neri kaandhukindra pai aravu udhari veezhththu padaippudan paanthal patrum veyya vegaththaal veezha munnam vegaththaal eythi koydha ikkuruththai sendru koduppan endru odi vandhaan. Porundhiya vida vegaththil podhuvaan vegam muntha varundhiye anaiyum pozhudhu maasunnam kavarndhadhu yaarukkum arunthavar amudhu seyya thaazhkaiyan araiye endru thirundhiya karuththinodum sezhumanai sendru pukkaan. Erividam muraiye eri thalaikkonda ezhaam vegam therivura eyirum kannum meniyum karugi theendhu viriyurai kulari aavi vidak kondu mayangi veezhvaan parikkalak kuruththai thaayaar paal vaiththu padimel veezhndhaan." Sekkizhar Peruman sings about the actions of the elder Thirunavukkarasu in three verses: "vandhaan" (he came), "pukkaan" (he entered), "veezhndhaan" (he fell). The snake that bit the elder Thirunavukkarasu's hand did not stop there but coiled around his hand, with its eyes burning with anger. Just as the heat of a fire affects those near it, the heat of the angry fire from the snake's eyes blazed like a fiery wind. The snake bit the elder Thirunavukkarasu, thinking he was trying to harm it, and coiled around his hand. Moreover, its anger did not subside, and the fire of anger continued to blaze in its eyes. Generally, snakes bite out of fear and anger for self-defense. Otherwise, they do not usually bite. Their venomous fangs naturally fold back into the upper jaw. So, they are not prone to biting. When these snakes feel fear or anger, their folded venomous fangs straighten up. Then, through the hollow of their tube-like venomous fangs, the venom from the venom sac at the base of the fangs is injected into the bodies of others, causing harm with their sharp fangs. The venom from the venom sac at the base of the snake's fangs passes through the hollow fangs and enters the body of the bitten person through the puncture made by the bite. The venom then mixes with their blood and enters their system. This is the general characteristic of venomous snakes. Some snakes not only bite but also coil around. This is also a special characteristic of some types of venomous snakes. The elder Thirunavukkarasu, realizing that the snake had coiled around his hand, shook his hand very quickly. As he shook it, the snake slipped and fell down. Due to this sudden and rapid danger, the elder Thirunavukkarasu's body trembled. He became greatly agitated. Thinking, "Before I fall due to the terrible speed of the poison caused by the snakebite, I will quickly go and deliver this banana leaf I plucked," the elder Thirunavukkarasu runs very fast. He thinks, "Even if I am bitten by a snake, it doesn't matter. No obstacle should occur to Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's sacred service. So, I will go very quickly and give this banana leaf." Thus, he goes very quickly. His bravery, mental fortitude, determination in action, and depth of love are conveyed here. He goes faster than the speed of the poison. As he goes fast, his blood circulation increases greatly. This increases the severity and suffering of the poison mixed in his blood. Therefore, he starts running, thinking, "I will go and deliver it quickly before I fall." He also thinks that he will not tell his parents that he was bitten by a snake, and with that determination, he quickly enters the house. This is because if he tells his parents that he was bitten by a snake, they will try to treat him. That will cause delay. There will be a delay in Thirunavukkarasu Peruman partaking of the sacred food. That will result in a sin against Shiva. Therefore, he thinks, "That sin against Shiva should not happen because of me. So, I will not tell anyone about this," and with his mind focused on his love for the devotee, he quickly goes home. He resolves not to tell anyone, not just his parents, even in that situation. At the same time he enters the house, the poison in his body reached the seventh stage, as Sekkizhar Peruman mentions. He sings, "Erividam muraiye eri thalaikkonda ezhaam vegam therivura." Poison burns. What does it burn? It destroys the vitality from the seven dhatus (constituents) in the body. Sekkizhar Peruman refers to this as the seventh stage because it destroys the nature of these seven dhatus. The spread of poison through each dhatu is considered the first stage, second stage, and so on, and destroying the seventh dhatu is called the seventh stage. Sekkizhar Peruman mentions it here. The seven dhatus in the body are tvak (skin), rakta (blood), mamsa (flesh), meda (fat), asthi (bone), and the seventh dhatu, shukla (semen). These seven, starting from blood, are interconnected. The poison spreads in this interconnected manner. First, in the blood, then that blood circulation goes into the heart, and from there, the poison spreads throughout the body one, two, three times. Thus, each time it spreads, the poison spreads in each dhatu: skin, muscle, fat, bone, in each circulation. In the seventh stage, it reaches its peak. In each circulation, that is, in each round of blood circulation, one dhatu is destroyed, and thus, when the blood circulates seven times in the body, all seven dhatus are destroyed.

Due to the completion of the seven stages, all seven dhatus become toxic and turn black. That black color also begins to appear externally. In that seventh round, the teeth, eyes, and body begin to blacken due to the advanced poison, so a black color appears. After this, consciousness is lost, and life ends. Thus, the elder Thirunavukkarasu reaches the seventh stage. The state of nearing death has arrived. Even though the state of nearing death has arrived, the elder Thirunavukkarasu stops that life from leaving with his determination. He possesses such mental strength. He was determined and resolved that he must deliver the plucked banana leaf and hold onto his life until then. Thus, the elder Thirunavukkarasu, who entered the house, placed the banana leaf he had plucked with his mother and then, overcome by poison, collapsed. He fell to the ground. As soon as he gave the banana leaf to his mother, he fainted because the nature of the nerves and other dhatus supporting the body was destroyed. Therefore, his body fell down. It is said that he "placed" the banana leaf with his mother, not "gave" it. That was his action in that state as he was falling. So, he extends the hand holding the banana leaf towards his mother, unable to give it properly. He stumbles and falls before his mother can take the leaf. So, he places the leaf with his mother and then stumbles and falls. The elder Thirunavukkarasu falls to the ground, thinking that he has completed the task assigned to him by his parents. Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 09 — Appoothi Adikal Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 512. Thirunindra Sarukkam, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar Puranam. The next verse composed by Sekkizhar Peruman is: "Thalarndhu veezh maganaik kandu thaayarum thandhaiyaarum ulam padhaiththu utru nokki udhiram sor vadivum meni vilangiya kuriyum kandu vidaththinaal veendhaan endru thulangudhal indri thondar amudhu seivatharkkuch choozhvaar." The elder Thirunavukkarasu places the banana leaf he had plucked on his mother's lap and falls down. His mother and father see the elder Thirunavukkarasu falling weakly. This situation is one where ordinary people in the world would be greatly distressed and lose their composure. But here, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar and his wife were steadfast in their devotion to the Lord. As it is said, "Bhootham ainthum nilaiyil kalanginum maathorubagar malarththaal marappilar odhu kaathal uraippil neri nindraar" (Even if the five elements waver, they will not forget the lotus feet of the Lord who has the Goddess as part, they stood firm in the path of intense love). Whatever the situation, whatever came, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar and his wife were in a state superior to those worldly people caught in illusion, who would be distressed and lose their composure; they were unwavering in their love for Shiva. According to the proverb "Petra manam piththu" (A mother's heart is mad), in a worldly, mundane sense, when they saw their son fall, the mother and father would be the first to be greatly agitated and suffer. So, here, as soon as they saw their son fall unconscious, their hearts were agitated as a natural reaction of primal love in a worldly state. Nevertheless, the elder Thirunavukkarasu was one who had entered into and performed service to the Lord's devotees. He was one who was yet to perform service to the Lord and His devotees. Therefore, the feeling that this suffering had come to him was what increased their agitation. They were distressed that one who had performed and was yet to perform the Lord's service and devotee's service had fallen in such distress. That is why they look to see how this suffering came to him. In the householder's life, such worldly love will be found in our great devotees who lived righteously. However, even such worldly love found here and there will not be an obstacle to the acts of devotion to Shiva by our Nayanmars. It will only be a support for them. This is the specialty of our Nayanmars. So, they see him fall. They look at the hand with which he brought the banana leaf. Blood is oozing from it. There are two holes on the back of that hand, as if pricked by a needle. Blood is seeping from them. Seeing this, they conclude that he has suffered from a snakebite. Nevertheless, their minds did not change or waver. Their devotion to Shiva, which they had for Shiva's devotees, did not diminish even a little. Without mental agitation, they think, "Now let's see to the arrangements for Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, the devotee, to partake of the sacred food." The next verse composed by Sekkizhar Peruman is: "Peralarum pudhalvan thannai paayinul peythu moodi puramanai muntril paangor pudaiyinil maraiththu vaiththe araiyidhu theriya vannam amudhu seivippom endru viraludai thondanarpaal viruppudan viraindhu vandhaar." They place their son, the elder Thirunavukkarasu, who is a rare son, a son full of greatness, in a mat, cover him, and hide him in a corner of the courtyard outside the house, so that no one can see him. This conveys the depth of love the parents had, their exalted love for Shiva's devotees, and their noble, righteous conduct of not allowing even the slightest delay or hindrance in the sacred service of devotion to Shiva. But for ordinary people, all this is utterly impossible.

This is an act that others cannot even imagine. The truth is explained here that all worldly illusory matters are set aside before actions performed with love for Shiva. They do not let anyone know that their son fell due to poison and that they hid his body. Now, they go there quickly with the desire to make the devotee Thirunavukkarasu Peruman partake of the sacred food, without letting anyone know. Generally, in a house where a death has occurred, 'asoucha' (ritual impurity) practices are observed. It is a tradition prescribed by moral texts that good feasts and offerings of sacred food should not be made in a house where a death has occurred. Our Appoothi Adikal Nayanar was glorified as "Appoothi Adikal, the exalted Brahmin." Therefore, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar was a great Brahmin with excellent Vedic discipline. But now, in this situation, with his eldest son having fallen thus in his house, he has dared to offer sacred food to Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, the holy devotee. Can this be considered acting contrary to the discipline explained by the 'asoucha' rules mentioned above? No, it is not. These 'asoucha' rules and regulations were established to maintain order in this world. Their benefits are limited to that extent. But here, our Nayanar undertook a devotion to Shiva that transcends all worldly states and is superior to all worldly disciplines, a devotion that grants ultimate benefit to the soul. Therefore, all worldly rules of discipline are to be set aside before devotion to Shiva. These general rules of discipline cannot stand before devotion to Shiva. Therefore, one cannot measure the events of the depth of devotion to Shiva with these worldly disciplines. Ordinary people cannot understand the exalted state of our Nayanmars. Thus, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar and his wife completely forget that such an event occurred, and their minds are entirely filled with the desire to offer sacred food to Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, the holy devotee. Therefore, they rush to satisfy the devotee's hunger. Sekkizhar Peruman describes the subsequent events in three consecutive verses. These are verses 30, 31, and 32 in the Puranam of Appoothi Adikal Nayanar. "Kadithu vandhu amudhu seyya kaalam thaazhgindradhe endre adisillum kariyum ellam azhagura anaiya vaiththu padiyil seer thondanar mun panindhu ezhundhu amudhu seythu em kudi muzhuvadhum uyyakkolveer endru avar koora kettarunthavar ezhundhu seyya adiyinai vilakki veror thirundhum aasanaththil eri parikalan thiruththum munnar irundhu venniru saaththi iyalbudaiya iruvarukkum porundhiya neer nalki pudhalvarkkum alikkum pozhdhil aadhi naan marai nool vaaimai Appoothiyaarai nokki kaathalar ivarkku mooththa seyaiyum kaattum munne methagu poothi saaththar endralum vilaindha thanmai yaadhum ondru uraiyar ippozhudhu ingu avan udavaan endraar." Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, seeing Thirunavukkarasu Peruman coming to his house, requested him to partake of the sacred food, obtained his consent, prepared six-taste food, and then after the incident with his son, all these things caused a long delay for Thirunavukkarasu Peruman to partake of the sacred food. Thinking that there should be no further delay, they quickly bring all the food and curries and place them beautifully near the eating place for Thirunavukkarasu Peruman to partake of the sacred food. They bring all the vessels and arrange them beautifully. They also arrange water and other necessary items beautifully and neatly. Having done this, they prostrate before Thirunavukkarasu Peruman and request, "Please partake of the sacred food and save our entire lineage." It is our tradition to make such a request when offering sacred food to Maheswaras, who are enlightened souls. They request, "Please partake of the sacred food here for the salvation of our entire lineage." In Shaiva tradition, the Shiva merit performed by one person in a lineage yields benefits for 21 generations in that lineage. "Moovezh sutram muranuru naragidai aazhame arul arase potri" (Praise be to the King of Grace, who saves 21 generations from falling into fierce hell) is from Thiruvachagam. Similarly, Arunachala Puranam also states that those who see or hear the Karthigai Deepam in Karthigai will be granted liberation for 21 generations in their lineage. Here, they request, "Please save our entire lineage." Through this, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar and his wife also imply that their entire lineage, including their son who lies fallen due to poison, should be saved. According to the proverb, "Naanum ariyēn avalum poy sollāḷ" (I don't know, and she doesn't lie), this request falls from Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's sacred utterance unknowingly, by the grace of Lord Shiva. As soon as they make this request, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, the great ascetic, cleanses his sacred feet. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's sacred feet had already been cleansed by Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, foot worship performed, and now the Lord is seated on the asana. Nevertheless, according to the rule of cleansing feet before partaking of sacred food, Navukkarasu Peruman cleansed his feet and hands. After cleansing his feet and hands, he sits on another asana. Before arranging the leaf and cleansing it, he applies sacred ash. It is a rule to apply sacred ash before and after partaking of sacred food. Thus, after applying sacred ash, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman offers sacred ash to Appoothi Adikal Nayanar and his wife. It is a good practice in Shaiva tradition for devotees to offer sacred ash before Maheswara Puja. This also becomes the cause for the events that follow in the history of Appoothi Adikal Nayanar. After offering sacred ash to both, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman says to Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, "All your other sons are here. So, bring the eldest son here too. Show the eldest son to apply sacred ash." Sekkizhar Peruman states that he says this to Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, who is described as "Aadhi naan marai nool vaaimai Appoothiyaarai." He says this to Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, who lives according to the truth of the ancient four Vedas and the Shruti texts associated with them. The truth spoken as the highest dharma in the Vedas is "Satyam Vada" (Speak the truth). Appoothi Adikal Nayanar is a great person who does not deviate from the truth of "Satyam Vada" prescribed by the Vedas, just as he does not deviate from the path of love, which is devotion to Shiva. Therefore, he says this. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, who graciously offered sacred ash to the elders, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar and his wife, asks them to call the elder Thirunavukkarasu to apply sacred ash to the children. It is a tradition for elders to apply sacred ash to children with their sacred hands and bless them. Therefore, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman asks them to show the elder son before applying sacred ash to the younger children. Hearing this from Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar says nothing about the events that transpired, but only says, "Now he is not available here." This indeed proves to be a truthful statement. Some may question whether it is right for Appoothi Adikal Nayanar to conceal the truth here. Sekkizhar Peruman, anticipating such questions, has already sung that Appoothi Adikal Nayanar had eradicated all faults such as theft, falsehood, lust, and anger. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar says, "Now he is not available here." Therefore, there is an implication that he will be available later. By saying that he is not available at this moment, this statement becomes a truthful one, indicating that he will be available later at the temple, as the events that follow will reveal. As soon as Thirunavukkarasu Peruman hears Appoothi Adikal Nayanar say, "Now he is not available here," a disturbance arises in Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's sacred mind due to divine grace. Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 10 — Appoothi Adikal Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 513. Thirunindra Sarukkam, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar Puranam. When Thirunavukkarasu Peruman asks where the elder Thirunavukkarasu is, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar replies, "Now he is not available here." Hearing that, a disturbance arises in Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's sacred mind due to divine grace. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman asks, "Something must have happened here. What did he do? What happened here? Please tell me the truth in detail." Thirunavukkarasu Peruman is the Peruman who held "Thirunindra Semmaiye" (righteousness that stands firm) as righteousness. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman is the Peruman who is absorbed in the nature of Shiva. Thirunavukkarasu Nayanar is a gracious one who remains unwavering, undisturbed by anything that happens. If a disturbance arises in his heart, it arises due to divine grace. He was giving sacred ash to everyone, and the elder Thirunavukkarasu should have come to receive it in due course, according to justice. But he did not come. Moreover, even when Thirunavukkarasu Peruman himself asked to show him to apply sacred ash, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar says, "Now he is not available here." Therefore, thinking that there is some hidden reason for this, he asks Appoothi Adikal Nayanar to explain what happened in detail. What Appoothi Adikal Nayanar said before was also true. Nevertheless, the truth was hidden there. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman asks him to elaborate. Hearing that, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar trembles in body and mind, and feels fear. Why? Because he fears that the opportunity for the great Thirunavukkarasu Peruman to partake of the sacred food might be lost. He trembles in mind. He trembles in body. Sekkizhar Peruman describes what is happening now in these two verses: "Avvurrai kettapothe Anganar arulaal anbar sevviya thiruvullaththu or thadumaatram sera nokki ivvurrai poraadhu en ullam en seythaan idharku ondru undaal mei viriththu uraiyum enna vilambuvaar vithirpputru anji periyavar amudhu seyyum peru idhu pizhaikka enno varuvadhu endru uraiyaarenum maathavar vinava vaaimai therivura uraiikka vendum seelaththaal sindhai nondhu parivodu vanangi maintharku utrathu pagarnthaar andre." Thirunavukkarasu Peruman asks him to explain what truly happened in detail. If he explains what happened fully, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman will not partake of the sacred food. He will tell them to perform the necessary rites for the deceased son. Therefore, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar fears that they will lose this blessing of having him partake of the sacred food now. When Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, the great ascetic, asked him to explain in detail, he should explain the truth very clearly as he asked. This is virtue. This is righteousness. This is the rule of conduct. Therefore, two duties now stand before Appoothi Adikal. If Thirunavukkarasu Peruman is to partake of the sacred food in his house, he must not tell what happened. But when he asked him to tell the truth, to explain what happened in detail, he must stand by the virtuous path of telling the truth. Both of these are difficult. Even though both are difficult, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar thinks that telling what happened as it happened is the best virtue to be done here. Similarly, in the previous Puranam of Karaikkal Ammaiyar, when Ammaiyar faced a similar dilemma between two dharmas, she tells her husband what happened, by the virtue that telling what happened as it happened is her duty. Recalling that here, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar also decides to tell what happened as it happened in detail. He says this with fear that if he tells, the blessing of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman partaking of the sacred food in his house will slip away from him. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar stands there, concealing everything that happened, even his son's death, desiring that Thirunavukkarasu Peruman should partake of the sacred food in his house. Is it appropriate for Appoothi Adikal Nayanar to offer sacred food in a house with the ritual impurity of his son's death? Are these actions that Appoothi Adikal should do? Should Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, a great Brahmin of excellent conduct, do this? Should Appoothi Adikal Nayanar conceal the news and do this to offer sacred food to the great one whom he considers his worshipped deity, in a house with ritual impurity? Many people here will research these questions. If one understands the depth of Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's devotion to Shiva, these questions will not arise. Offering sacred food to an excellent Shiva devotee is like offering sacred food to Lord Shiva himself. It is an excellent 'pathi punniyam' (merit related to the Lord) that ensures the well-being of the soul. But 'asoucham' (ritual impurity) is related to the body. It is limited to the physical level and is related to maya. Therefore, before excellent 'pathi punniyam', these events related to the nature of attachment become nothing. "Yaavaiyum sooniyam saththu edhir aadhallin" (All are void before existence) is from Sivagnanabodham. "Munaiththidaadhu asaththu saththin mun irul ravi mun pola" (Non-existence does not manifest before existence, like darkness before the sun) is from Sivagnana Siddhiyar. According to the principle that non-existence does not shine before existence, in the heart of Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, which is filled with the nature of 'pathi punniyam', events related to the lower nature of attachment will not arise. Nevertheless, why did Appoothi Adikal Nayanar conceal this from Thirunavukkarasu Peruman? Because Appoothi Adikal Nayanar was a Brahmin who had attained a state of knowledge transcending this world. That is why Sekkizhar Peruman referred to him as "Jnana Thirumaraiyor" (Enlightened Sacred Brahmin) in verse 1469. Yet, he did not realize that he had attained a state transcending these worlds. That was his greatness. He realized it when Thirunavukkarasu Peruman showed his grace. Therefore, his not realizing his own greatness and his not realizing that Thirunavukkarasu Peruman would realize it were the reasons why Appoothi Adikal Nayanar concealed what happened. Therefore, in this sacred history of Periyapuranam, there is no place for low mental emotions like deceit, deception, etc., that arise from the tamasic quality at a worldly, mundane level. If one reads by internalizing that anger, deceit, deception, etc., that arise from tamo-guna, will not approach any true Nayanmars even a little, then the true state of this sacred history will be somewhat understood. Therefore, research done by worldly people, clinging to their lower state, will only result in transgression and will not reveal the truth. That is why one should humbly learn and understand all these truths and their natures from experienced gurus. Thus, Sivakavimani Ayya elaborates here. In accordance with Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's request, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar describes what happened. As soon as Thirunavukkarasu Peruman heard Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's description, he said, "What you said is very good. Who in this world would do such a thing?" and looking at the body of the deceased son, he sings a melodious sacred hymn, praying for the Lord's grace. Sekkizhar Peruman composes this verse: "Navukku arasar kela nanru neer mozhindha vannam yaavar iththanmai seythaar endru mun ezhundhu sendru aavi theer savaththai nokki annalaar arul vannam pa isai padhigam paadi panividam paatruvitthaar." Thirunavukkarasu Peruman says, "What you did is good. Who in this world would do such a thing?" and looking at the body of the deceased son, he sings a hymn, praying for the Lord's grace. He asks them to bring the child in front of the temple, and has the child brought from Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's house and placed in front of the temple. With great compassion, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman sings a hymn, praying for the Lord's divine grace. More than the son falling due to poison, it was the act of Appoothi Adikal Nayanar and his wife concealing it that caused immeasurable compassion to bloom in Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's sacred heart. The compassion in Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's sacred heart overflowed not for the son, nor for Appoothi Adikal Nayanar who lost his son. This is because death is natural for living beings in this world. Losing a son is also natural. These are all the Lord's decrees. Therefore, great spiritual masters will not try to revive all the dead, nor will they try to prevent anyone from dying and remove all sufferings. Moreover, such attempts are the actions of those who do not know that it is the Lord's decree. But here, what caused great compassion in Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's sacred heart was the act of Appoothi Adikal Nayanar and his wife concealing this incident. This was done by them due to the abundance of their love for the devotee. Therefore, this is what caused compassion in Thirunavukkarasu Peruman's sacred heart. That is why Thirunavukkarasu Peruman removes this suffering. He has the body of the deceased child brought and placed in front of the temple of Lord Shiva, who has fragrant Konrai flowers in his matted hair. He sings a hymn, praying for the Lord's divine grace. Even if he had sung the hymn in Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's house, the Lord would have shown grace. But why does he sing the hymn in front of the temple? It is because this happens by the grace of the Lord, who bestows His nature upon His devotees. To make the people of the world know that this happens by the grace of the Lord, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman places the child in front of the temple and sings the hymn. Sekkizhar Peruman sings, "Aavi theer savaththai nokki annalaar arulum vannam pa isai padhigam paadi panividam paatruvitthaar." It is the nature of our great people to act according to the grace of Lord Shiva, without any ego, and to have all their actions happen as His actions. Therefore, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman sings a melodious hymn as the Lord bestows His grace. The hymn he now sang is the Thiruppathigam that begins with "Ondru Kōlām." In this Thiruppathigam, the greatness of the Lord is spoken of in forty ways, starting with "Ondru Kōlām," "Irandu Kōlām," and so on, up to "Paththu Kōlām." This Thiruppathigam is named "Vitam Theerndha Thiruppathigam" (The Hymn that Removed Poison). This hymn is a general hymn set in the Indalam tune. This hymn is structured with numerical embellishments, using "one, two, three," and so on. It is compiled as the 18th Thiruppathigam in the Fourth Thirumurai. Now, that Thiruppathigam. Thiruchitrambalam.
