"Ondru kolam avar sindhaiyuyar varai ondru kolam uyarum madhi sooduvar ondru kolam idu vendalai kaiyathu ondru kolam avar oorvadhu thaane Irandu kolam imaiyor thozhu paadham irandu kolam ilangum kuzhaip pennan irandu kolam uruvam siru maan mazhu irandu kolam avar eythina thaame Moondru kolam avar kannudhalavana moondru kolam avar soolaththin moy ilai moondru kolam kanai kaiyathu vinnan moondru kolam puram eythana thaame Naalu kolam avar tham mugam aavana naalu kolam sananam muthal thotramum naalu kolam avar poorththiyin paadhangal naalu kolam marai paadina thaame Anju kolam avar aadaravin padam anju kolam avar velpulanaavana anju kolam avar kaayappattaan kanai anju kolam avar aadana thaame Aaru kolam avar angam padaiththana aaru kolam avar tham maganaar mugam aaru kolam avar thaarmisai vanddin kaal aaru kolam suvai aakkina thaame Ezhu kolam avar oozhi padaiththana ezhu kolam avar kanda irungadal ezhu kolam avar aalum ulagangal ezhu kolam isai aakkina thaame Ettu kolam avar eeril perungunam ettu kolam avar soodum inamalar ettu kolam avar thol inaiyavana ettu kolam disai aakkina thaame Onbadhu polavar vaasal vaguththana onbadhu polavar maarbin noolizhai onbadhu polavar kolakkuzhal sadai onbadhu polavar paaridam thaane Paththu kolam avar paambin kan paambin pal paththu kolam eyirin eyirin thukkana paththu kolam avar kaayappattaan thalai paththu kolam adiyaar seygai thaane" Thiruchitrambalam. As soon as Thirunavukkarasu Peruman recited this Thiruppathigam that removed poison, the elder Thirunavukkarasu, the son of Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, rose as if waking from sleep. Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 11 — Appoothi Adikal Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 514. As soon as Thirunavukkarasu Peruman recited the Thiruppathigam, the elder Thirunavukkarasu rose as if waking from sleep. Earlier, when he fell unconscious due to poison, he fell with the thought that he would not tell anyone that the snake had bitten him, because if he did, there would be a delay in Thirunavukkarasu Peruman partaking of the sacred food, so he would not tell. Therefore, the elder Thirunavukkarasu, who now rises, rises continuing that sacred service and now bows to the sacred feet of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. Without even looking at his parents who are standing there, as soon as he rises, he first seeks and holds onto the sacred feet of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. Moral texts and spiritual texts state that death is like sleep, and birth is like waking up from sleep. Among the five cosmic functions performed by Lord Shiva, destruction and concealment are for the souls to rest, to attain karmic maturity and karmic equality, and thereby to remove impurities. This is the conclusion of spiritual texts. Accordingly, Sekkizhar Peruman states that the elder Thirunavukkarasu rises as if waking from sleep. As Sivagnanabodham states, "Saarndhaarai kaaththal thalaivar kadanaadhal" (It is the duty of the leader to protect those who seek refuge), Thirunavukkarasu Peruman sang and bestowed grace as Lord Shiva willed. Through that, the elder Thirunavukkarasu has now risen. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman bestows the exalted sacred ash upon the risen elder Thirunavukkarasu. This incident occurred because Thirunavukkarasu Peruman asked them to show their eldest son to apply sacred ash. Therefore, now that the elder Thirunavukkarasu has returned, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman bestows the sacred ash upon him. All who saw the child whose life had returned praised the greatness of Lord Shiva's divine sacred path. Through that, they too received great blessings; they received the blessing of standing in the path of Shiva. This is because they had directly witnessed the divine grace of Lord Shiva. Therefore, they were blessed to stand in the path of that sacred service. At the same time, even though their child had returned, what did the child's parents think? They lamented that he had caused a slight hindrance to their leader, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, partaking of the sacred food. Therefore, they also thought that they should quickly arrange for Thirunavukkarasu Peruman to partake of the sacred food. Parents are supposed to rejoice upon seeing their child come back to life, but instead, they were saddened because they thought he had caused a hindrance to Thirunavukkarasu Peruman partaking of the sacred food. The hindrance he caused was the delay; that is what they considered a hindrance. But the child did nothing to cause that delay. What did the child actually do? He ran and delivered the banana leaf to his mother before the poison could spread, so that there would be no delay. Not only that, he came with a firm mind, resolving not to tell anyone that the snake had bitten him, so that there would be no delay in Thirunavukkarasu Peruman partaking of the sacred food. Despite all this, the parents think, "He caused a hindrance; a delay occurred because of him," and lament. This is because they attribute the delay that occurred through him, through his actions, to him, and lament. This is due to the boundless love that Appoothi Adikal Nayanar and his wife had for Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. They say this due to their intense love for feeding Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. That is why they lament. But all these are things that ordinary people cannot understand. These are things that are contrary to ordinary worldly ways. This truth can only be understood by those who are elevated in the state of love, like Appoothi Adikal Nayanar. Therefore, these two things—that is, they even hid their son's death, wrapped his body in a mat, and proceeded to feed Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. Now, seeing their son come back to life, they do not rejoice but lament that a delay occurred because of him. These two are the extraordinary deeds performed by Appoothi Adikal Nayanar. These are the greatnesses of Appoothi Adikal Nayanar and his wife. These are things that other ordinary humans cannot do. They are extraordinary deeds that cannot be performed. Therefore, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, knowing their distress, quickly proceeds from the temple to their house to partake of the sacred food, even without them inviting him again. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar also makes all the arrangements for the sacred food with great joy. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar had already made a request, "Please partake of the sacred food. Please partake of the sacred food and save our entire lineage." Due to that, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman then sat on the asana to partake of the sacred food. Therefore, continuing that request, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman now proceeds to partake of the sacred food. They make arrangements for the Peruman to partake of the sacred food. They clean the place, the ground where the Peruman will partake of the sacred food, with sacred water mixed with cow dung. Among the five types of purification, this is called 'sthana suddhi' (purification of the place). This is an essential part of Shiva Puja. Therefore, similarly, it must be performed in Maheswara Puja. So, they clean the place with water mixed with cow dung. This cow dung is praised in Vedas and scriptures. This is because cow dung is the source for producing sacred ash. Cow dung is used for cleaning and purifying the temples where Lord Shiva resides. Not only that, cow dung is also one of the components of 'panchagavya' (five products of the cow). Therefore, they clean the place with water mixed with such special cow dung. They draw patterns with white chalk paste, that is, they draw patterns with dissolved flour. They light lamps. They light incense. Just as in Shiva Puja, incense and lamps are essential for Maheswara Puja. After making all these arrangements, they wash and clean the banana leaf thoroughly and place it there. Then, they place the leaf so that the side where the leaf is cut is to the right of the person eating. Here, Sekkizhar Peruman explains to our Shaiva devotees how Maheswara Puja should be performed. Sekkizhar Peruman describes through the actions of Appoothi Adikal Nayanar to explain and emphasize to the Shaiva devotees that cleaning the ground with cow dung water, drawing patterns, lighting lamps, preparing the serving plate, placing the serving plate, and offering good water for washing hands are all essential parts of Maheswara Puja in Shaiva tradition. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar offers good fragrant water for Thirunavukkarasu Peruman to wash his sacred hands. Washing hands before eating is a religious and ethical practice. It is also a practice of external purity. Moreover, the water used for washing hands and drinking should be good water. Water from springs, wells, rivers, and ponds would have been exposed to soil, air, etc. Other creatures would also have touched it. Therefore, all this water should be passed through sand, cloth, etc., to remove impurities. Then it should be placed in the sun to cool. These are the methods our ancestors discovered for purifying water. Not only that, fragrant roots like vetiver, flowers, green camphor, saffron, etc., should be added to that water. These are good substances that are not only for fragrance but also for medicinal properties. After Appoothi Adikal Nayanar does all this, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman washes his sacred hands with that good water and then, looking at Appoothi Adikal and his sons, graciously says, "You and your dear sons, please sit near me and partake of the sacred food." The compassionate gaze of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman looks at everyone with eyes of grace, bestows Shiva's grace, performs Shiva diksha, makes them Maheswaras, and settles there. Moreover, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman also addresses the sons as "dear sons." What is their dearness? They are special ones who are endowed with the quality of devotion to Shiva, befitting their parents. Therefore, they are praised by Thirunavukkarasu Peruman as "dear sons." Therefore, looking at their dearness, he first calls the sons and graciously says, "You and your sons, partake of the sacred food." Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 12 — Appoothi Adikal Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 515. Thirunindra Sarukkam, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar Puranam. As Thirunavukkarasu Peruman requested them to partake of the sacred food with him, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar and his sons sit with Thirunavukkarasu Peruman and partake of the sacred food. Along with them, all the devotees also join them, and Maheswara Puja takes place in Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's house. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's wife serves everyone with great desire and devotion. At this point, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar is a Brahmin, and Thirunavukkarasu Peruman is a Vellalar. Therefore, some people will research this event of all of them eating together, focusing on caste, lineage, and so on. But all these researches are unnecessary and should be discarded. This history occurred based on the elevated religious discipline of the soul concerning love for Shiva. Therefore, there is absolutely no connection between these and ordinary worldly customs and researches about caste and lineage. Thus, in Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's sacred house, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar's sons, and other Shiva devotees performed Maheswara Puja and partook of the sacred food. Appoothi Adikal Nayanar, the great ascetic Brahmin, had performed great penances before. He is still performing great penances. Therefore, as a result of these penances, Appoothi Adikal Nayanar received the great blessing of guru's grace. Because of this, his house became a house of wealth. Here, wealth refers to the wealth of liberation. "Selvan kazhal ettum selvame selvam" (The wealth of praising the feet of the wealthy one is true wealth) is a Devaram utterance. Such a house was the one where Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, who possessed the wealth of praising the feet of the wealthy one, who held "Thirunindra Semmaiye" as righteousness, himself graced and showed compassion. Therefore, it was a house of wealth. Thus, Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, after partaking of the sacred food in Appoothi Adikal Nayanar
